                                             Cilantro-Chili Pepper Sauce
Ingredients                       Directions
3 fresh red chile peppers        Cut the stem end off of the chile peppers, and remove the seeds
1 tablespoon sesame oil          using a thin knife, otherwise leaving the peppers whole. Place the
3 cloves garlic, minced          peppers into a skillet, and toast over medium-high heat, turning
1 pinch kosher salt              frequently, until the skins of the peppers have blackened and
1/2 cup malt vinegar             loosened, about 10 minutes. The peppers are ready when 1/4 of the
2 tablespoons fish sauce         skin has blackened. Remove the peppers, place into a small bowl,
2 tablespoons brown sugar        and cover with plastic wrap. Allow to cool and steam for about 15
                                 minutes, then remove and discard the skins. Chop the peppers, and
3 tablespoons soy sauce
                                 set aside.
1 teaspoon lime juice
1 bunch cilantro, chopped        Heat the sesame oil in the skillet over medium heat. Stir in the
1 green onion, chopped           garlic, and cook for 2 minutes to soften, then increase heat to
                                 medium-high, and stir in the chopped peppers and salt. Cook and
                                 stir until the mixture is hot and sizzling, about 1 minute. Pour in the
                                 vinegar, brown sugar, and fish sauce. Bring to a boil, then reduce
                                 heat to medium-low, and allow to simmer for 10 minutes.

                                 Scrape the mixture into a blender, and add the soy sauce, lime
                                 juice, cilantro, and green onion. Puree until smooth. Serve
                                 immediately.
